empty:hidden mb-14" data-page-title="Track cost and usage" data-page-href="/en/agent-sdk/cost-tracking" id="content"><span data-as="p">The Claude Agent SDK provides detailed token usage information for each interaction with Claude. This guide explains how to properly track usage and understand cost reporting, especially when dealing with parallel tool uses and multi-step conversations.</span>
<span data-as="p">For complete API documentation, see the <a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/typescript" class="link">TypeScript SDK reference</a> and <a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/python" class="link">Python SDK reference</a>.</span>
<span id="estimates-not-billing"></span>
<div role="note" aria-label="Warning" class="callout my-4 px-5 py-4 overflow-hidden rounded-2xl flex gap-3 border border-yellow-200 bg-yellow-50 dark:border-yellow-900 dark:bg-yellow-600/20 [&amp;_[data-component-part=&#x27;callout-icon&#x27;]]:mt-px" data-callout-type="warning"><div class="mt-0.5 w-4" data-component-part="callout-icon"><svg aria-hidden="true" width="18" height="18" stroke-width="1.5" class="flex-none size-5 text-yellow-800 dark:text-yellow-300"><use href="#mi-mint-1-CalloutWarning"></use></svg></div><div class="text-sm prose dark:prose-invert min-w-0 w-full [&amp;_kbd]:bg-background-light dark:[&amp;_kbd]:bg-background-dark [&amp;_code]:text-current! [&amp;_kbd]:text-current! [&amp;_a]:text-current! [&amp;_a]:border-current [&amp;_strong]:text-current! text-yellow-800 dark:text-yellow-300" data-component-part="callout-content"><span data-as="p">The <code>total_cost_usd</code> and <code>costUSD</code> fields are client-side estimates, not authoritative billing data. The SDK computes them locally from a price table bundled at build time, unless a <a href="/docs/en/settings-reference#modelpricing" class="link"><code>modelPricing</code></a> table is in effect. They can drift from what you are actually billed when:</span><ul>
<li>pricing changes</li>
<li>the installed SDK version does not recognize a model</li>
<li>billing rules apply that the client cannot model</li>
</ul><span data-as="p">One billing rule the SDK does model is <a href="https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/about-claude/pricing#data-residency-pricing" class="link" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer">data residency pricing</a>. When a response’s <code>usage</code> reports <code>inference_geo: &quot;us&quot;</code>, the SDK multiplies the list price of that response’s tokens by 1.1. Per-request fees such as web search aren’t multiplied. Requires TypeScript Agent SDK v0.3.239 or later, or Python Agent SDK v0.2.144 or later.</span><span data-as="p">Use these fields for development insight and approximate budgeting. For authoritative billing, see <a href="/docs/en/costs#manage-costs-for-your-organization" class="link">Manage costs for your organization</a>. Don’t bill end users or trigger financial decisions from these fields.</span></div></div>
<h2 class="flex whitespace-pre-wrap group font-semibold" id="understand-token-usage" aria-labelledby="_R_99deehkd74t9_"><a href="#understand-token-usage" class="anchor-link" aria-label="Navigate to header: Understand token usage"></a><span id="_R_99deehkd74t9_" class="cursor-pointer">Understand token usage</span></h2>
<span data-as="p">The TypeScript and Python SDKs expose the same usage data with different field names:</span>
<ul>
<li><strong>TypeScript</strong> provides per-step token breakdowns on each assistant message (<code>message.message.id</code>, <code>message.message.usage</code>), per-model cost via <code>modelUsage</code> on the result message, and a cumulative total on the result message.</li>
<li><strong>Python</strong> provides per-step token breakdowns on each assistant message as <code>message.usage</code> and <code>message.message_id</code>, per-model cost via <code>model_usage</code> on the result message, and the cumulative total on the result message as <code>total_cost_usd</code>.</li>
</ul>
<span data-as="p">Both SDKs use the same underlying cost model and expose the same granularity. The difference is in field naming and where per-step usage is nested.</span>
<span data-as="p">Cost tracking depends on understanding how the SDK scopes usage data:</span>
<ul>
<li><strong><code>query()</code> call:</strong> one invocation of the SDK’s <code>query()</code> function. A single call can involve multiple steps: Claude responds, uses tools, gets results, and responds again. Each call produces one <a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/typescript#sdkresultmessage" class="link"><code>result</code></a> message at the end, except in <a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/streaming-vs-single-mode" class="link">streaming input mode</a>, where one <code>query()</code> call carries multiple user turns and each turn emits its own <code>result</code> message.</li>
<li><strong>Step:</strong> a single request/response cycle within a <code>query()</code> call. Each step produces assistant messages with token usage.</li>
<li><strong>Session:</strong> a series of <code>query()</code> calls linked by a session ID through the <code>resume</code> option. A resumed call’s results report the session’s whole spend, not just that call’s own. See <a href="#accumulate-costs-across-multiple-calls" class="link">Accumulate costs across multiple calls</a> for how the totals carry over.</li>
</ul>
<span data-as="p">The following diagram shows the message stream from a single <code>query()</code> call, with token usage reported at each step and the cumulative estimate at the end:</span>

Step 1 has four assistant messages sharing the same ID and usage (count once), Step 2 has one assistant message with a new ID, and the final result message shows the estimated total_cost_usd." <div role="list" class="steps ml-3.5 mt-10 mb-6"><div role="listitem" class="step group/step step-container relative flex items-start pb-5"><div data-component-part="step-line" class="absolute w-px h-[calc(100%-2.75rem)] top-11 bg-gray-200/70 dark:bg-white/10" contentEditable="false"></div><div class="absolute ml-[-13px] py-2" data-component-part="step-number" contentEditable="false"><div data-component-part="step-indicator" class="group/step-indicator relative size-7 shrink-0 rounded-full bg-gray-50 dark:bg-white/10 text-xs text-gray-900 dark:text-gray-50 font-semibold flex items-center justify-center"><div class="[[data-component-part=step-indicator]:has(a:focus-visible)_&amp;]:opacity-0">1</div></div></div><div class="w-full overflow-hidden pl-8 pr-px"><p class="mt-2 font-semibold prose dark:prose-invert text-gray-900 dark:text-gray-200" contentEditable="false" data-component-part="step-title">Each step produces assistant messages</p><div data-component-part="step-content" class="prose dark:prose-invert"><span data-as="p">When Claude responds, it sends one or more assistant messages. In TypeScript, each assistant message contains a nested <code>BetaMessage</code> (accessed via <code>message.message</code>) with an <code>id</code> and a <a href="https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/api/messages" class="link" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer"><code>usage</code></a> object with token counts (<code>input_tokens</code>, <code>output_tokens</code>). In Python, the <code>AssistantMessage</code> dataclass exposes the same data directly via <code>message.usage</code> and <code>message.message_id</code>. When Claude uses multiple tools in one turn, all messages in that turn share the same ID, so deduplicate by ID to avoid double-counting.</span></div></div></div><div role="listitem" class="step group/step step-container relative flex items-start pb-5"><div data-component-part="step-line" class="absolute w-px h-[calc(100%-2.75rem)] top-11 bg-transparent bg-linear-to-b from-gray-200 dark:from-white/10 via-80% to-transparent [.step-container:has([data-component-part=step-content]:empty)_&amp;]:hidden" contentEditable="false"></div><div class="absolute ml-[-13px] py-2" data-component-part="step-number" contentEditable="false"><div data-component-part="step-indicator" class="group/step-indicator relative size-7 shrink-0 rounded-full bg-gray-50 dark:bg-white/10 text-xs text-gray-900 dark:text-gray-50 font-semibold flex items-center justify-center"><div class="[[data-component-part=step-indicator]:has(a:focus-visible)_&amp;]:opacity-0">2</div></div></div><div class="w-full overflow-hidden pl-8 pr-px"><p class="mt-2 font-semibold prose dark:prose-invert text-gray-900 dark:text-gray-200" contentEditable="false" data-component-part="step-title">The result message provides the cumulative estimate</p><div data-component-part="step-content" class="prose dark:prose-invert"><span data-as="p">When the <code>query()</code> call completes, the SDK emits a result message with <code>total_cost_usd</code> and cumulative <code>usage</code>, typed as <a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/typescript#sdkresultmessage" class="link"><code>SDKResultMessage</code></a> in TypeScript and <a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/python#resultmessage" class="link"><code>ResultMessage</code></a> in Python. If you only need the estimated total, you can ignore the per-step usage and read this single value.</span><span data-as="p">If you make multiple independent <code>query()</code> calls, each result reflects only the cost of that individual call. A call that resumes a session also counts the session’s earlier spend.</span><span data-as="p">In streaming input mode, each turn emits its own result message. See <a href="#track-costs-in-streaming-input-mode" class="link">Track costs in streaming input mode</a> for how to read call totals in that mode.</span></div></div></div></div>
<h2 class="flex whitespace-pre-wrap group font-semibold" id="track-costs-in-streaming-input-mode" aria-labelledby="_R_t9deehkd74t9_"><a href="#track-costs-in-streaming-input-mode" class="anchor-link" aria-label="Navigate to header: Track costs in streaming input mode"></a><span id="_R_t9deehkd74t9_" class="cursor-pointer">Track costs in streaming input mode</span></h2>
<span data-as="p">In <a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/streaming-vs-single-mode" class="link">streaming input mode</a>, one <code>query()</code> call carries multiple user turns and each turn emits its own result message. The result fields differ in scope:</span>
<ul>
<li><strong><code>usage</code></strong>: covers only that turn, and within it only the main agent loop, not any subagents it ran.</li>
<li><strong><code>total_cost_usd</code> and <code>modelUsage</code>, or <code>model_usage</code> in Python</strong>: carry the running total for the whole call so far, plus any spend restored when the call resumed a session.</li>
</ul>
<span data-as="p">In a call where your app never sends <code>/clear</code>, <code>/reset</code>, or <code>/new</code>, read the latest result for call totals rather than summing across results.</span>
<span data-as="p">The running totals start over each time your app sends one of those three commands, and inside a <code>query()</code> call nothing else resets them. Three results matter for your accounting:</span>
<ul>
<li><strong>The <code>/clear</code> turn’s own result</strong>: covers only what has run since the reset, and carries a new <code>session_id</code>.</li>
<li><strong>Every later result</strong>: keeps counting from that reset.</li>
<li><strong>The last result before each <code>/clear</code></strong>: holds the total for the turns since the previous reset.</li>
</ul>
<span data-as="p">To total the whole call, add the last result from before each <code>/clear</code> to the call’s final result. Every other result, including the <code>/clear</code> turn’s own, is superseded by a later one.</span>
<span data-as="p">In TypeScript, the SDK also emits an <a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/typescript#sdkconversationresetmessage" class="link"><code>SDKConversationResetMessage</code></a> at each reset, so you can detect resets from the stream. In Python, the SDK likewise emits a <code>ConversationResetMessage</code>. Before Python SDK v0.2.137, the Python iterator dropped that message, so on those versions count the resets yourself from the <code>/clear</code> turns your app sends.</span>
<span data-as="p"><code>maxBudgetUsd</code> (TypeScript) or <code>max_budget_usd</code> (Python) counts only the call’s own spend: totals restored from a resumed session don’t count against it, and a <code>/clear</code> starts the budget over.</span>
<h2 class="flex whitespace-pre-wrap group font-semibold" id="get-the-total-cost-of-a-query" aria-labelledby="_R_1f9deehkd74t9_"><a href="#get-the-total-cost-of-a-query" class="anchor-link" aria-label="Navigate to header: Get the total cost of a query"></a><span id="_R_1f9deehkd74t9_" class="cursor-pointer">Get the total cost of a query</span></h2>
<span data-as="p">The result message, typed as <a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/typescript#sdkresultmessage" class="link"><code>SDKResultMessage</code></a> in TypeScript and <a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/python#resultmessage" class="link"><code>ResultMessage</code></a> in Python, marks the end of the agent loop for a <code>query()</code> call. It includes <code>total_cost_usd</code>, the cumulative estimated cost across all steps in that call. A call that resumes a session also counts the session’s earlier spend. Two caveats apply when you read the value:</span>
<ul>
<li>In Python the field is typed as optional, so check that it isn’t <code>None</code> before you read it.</li>
<li>Success and error results both carry it, though the final result of a <a href="#recover-totals-after-a-session-crash" class="link">session crash</a> may carry it zeroed.</li>
</ul>
<span data-as="p">In streaming input mode, read call totals as described in <a href="#track-costs-in-streaming-input-mode" class="link">Track costs in streaming input mode</a>.</span>
<span data-as="p">The three result-level fields differ in what they count when the agent spawns <a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/subagents" class="link">subagents</a>. Use <code>modelUsage</code>, or <code>model_usage</code> in Python, for whole-tree token accounting; the <code>usage</code> field undercounts as soon as nesting occurs.</span>
<!-- --><div role="presentation" style="position:relative;--scroll-area-corner-height:0px;--scroll-area-corner-width:0px" data-component-part="scroll-area" data-table-wrapper="true" class="min-w-0 [--page-padding:20px] flex w-[calc(100%+(var(--page-padding)*2))] my-[1em] py-[1em] -mx-(--page-padding) max-w-none [contain:inline-size]"><div data-id="base-ui-_R_1p9deehkd74t9_-viewport" tabindex="-1" class="size-full rounded-[inherit] [--scroll-area-fade-size:32px] overflow-y-hidden! base-ui-disable-scrollbar" style="overflow:scroll" data-component-part="scroll-area-viewport" role="region" aria-label="Scrollable table"><div role="presentation" style="min-width:fit-content" data-component-part="scroll-area-content" class="flex"><div class="px-(--page-padding) grow max-w-none table"><table class="m-0 min-w-full w-full max-w-none table [&amp;_th]:text-left [&amp;_td[data-numeric]]:tabular-nums [&amp;_td]:min-w-[150px]"><thead><tr><th>Field</th><th>Subagent activity</th></tr></thead><tbody><tr><td><code>usage</code></td><td>Excluded. Counts only the top-level agent loop, so tokens consumed inside subagents are not added</td></tr><tr><td><code>total_cost_usd</code></td><td>Included. Counts subagent requests alongside the top-level loop</td></tr><tr><td><code>modelUsage</code> / <code>model_usage</code></td><td>Included. Counts subagent requests alongside the top-level loop, broken down by model</td></tr></tbody></table></div></div></div></div>
<span data-as="p">In <a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/streaming-vs-single-mode#choose-single-shot-or-streaming-input" class="link">single message input mode</a>, Claude Code waits for background subagents still running at the end of the final turn before emitting the result. The wait lasts up to the cap described in <a href="/docs/en/headless#background-tasks-at-exit" class="link">background tasks at exit</a>. The result’s <code>total_cost_usd</code>, <code>duration_api_ms</code>, and <code>modelUsage</code>, or <code>model_usage</code> in Python, include the work done during that wait.</span>
<span data-as="p">The following examples iterate over the message stream from a <code>query()</code> call and print the total cost when the <code>result</code> message arrives:</span>

<span class="line"><span style="color:#6E7781;--shiki-dark:#6A9955">        # failure was an error result, the branch above has already run;</span></span>

<span class="line"><span style="color:#0550AE;--shiki-dark:#DCDCAA">        print</span><span style="color:#1F2328;--shiki-dark:#D4D4D4">(</span><span style="color:#CF222E;--shiki-dark:#569CD6">f</span><span style="color:#0A3069;--shiki-dark:#CE9178">&quot;Session ended with an error: </span><span style="color:#CF222E;--shiki-dark:#569CD6">{</span><span style="color:#1F2328;--shiki-dark:#D4D4D4">error</span><span style="color:#CF222E;--shiki-dark:#569CD6">}</span><span style="color:#0A3069;--shiki-dark:#CE9178">&quot;</span><span style="color:#1F2328;--shiki-dark:#D4D4D4">)</span></span>
<span class="line"></span>
<span class="line"></span>
<span class="line"><span style="color:#1F2328;--shiki-dark:#D4D4D4">asyncio.run(main())</span></span>
</code></pre></div></div></div></div><div class="code-block-fade-overlay print:hidden" data-fade-overlay="true" aria-hidden="true" style="--fade-color-light:#ffffff;--fade-color-dark:#0B0C0E"></div></div></div></div>
<span data-as="p">To bound how much subagents can add to <code>total_cost_usd</code>, set the <a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/subagents#cap-subagent-depth-concurrency-and-spend" class="link">depth, concurrency, and spend limits</a> on the query.</span>
<h2 class="flex whitespace-pre-wrap group font-semibold" id="track-per-step-and-per-model-usage" aria-labelledby="_R_239deehkd74t9_"><a href="#track-per-step-and-per-model-usage" class="anchor-link" aria-label="Navigate to header: Track per-step and per-model usage"></a><span id="_R_239deehkd74t9_" class="cursor-pointer">Track per-step and per-model usage</span></h2>
<span data-as="p">The examples in this section use TypeScript field names. In Python, the equivalent fields are <a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/python#assistantmessage" class="link"><code>AssistantMessage.usage</code></a> and <code>AssistantMessage.message_id</code> for per-step usage, and <a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/python#resultmessage" class="link"><code>ResultMessage.model_usage</code></a> for per-model breakdowns.</span>
<h3 class="flex whitespace-pre-wrap group font-semibold" id="track-per-step-usage" aria-labelledby="_R_279deehkd74t9_"><a href="#track-per-step-usage" class="anchor-link" aria-label="Navigate to header: Track per-step usage"></a><span id="_R_279deehkd74t9_" class="cursor-pointer">Track per-step usage</span></h3>
<span data-as="p">Each assistant message contains a nested <code>BetaMessage</code> (accessed via <code>message.message</code>) with an <code>id</code> and <code>usage</code> object with token counts. When Claude uses tools in parallel, multiple messages share the same <code>id</code> with identical usage data. Track which IDs you’ve already counted and skip duplicates to avoid inflated totals.</span>
<div role="note" aria-label="Warning" class="callout my-4 px-5 py-4 overflow-hidden rounded-2xl flex gap-3 border border-yellow-200 bg-yellow-50 dark:border-yellow-900 dark:bg-yellow-600/20 [&amp;_[data-component-part=&#x27;callout-icon&#x27;]]:mt-px" data-callout-type="warning"><div class="mt-0.5 w-4" data-component-part="callout-icon"><svg aria-hidden="true" width="18" height="18" stroke-width="1.5" class="flex-none size-5 text-yellow-800 dark:text-yellow-300"><use href="#mi-mint-1-CalloutWarning"></use></svg></div><div class="text-sm prose dark:prose-invert min-w-0 w-full [&amp;_kbd]:bg-background-light dark:[&amp;_kbd]:bg-background-dark [&amp;_code]:text-current! [&amp;_kbd]:text-current! [&amp;_a]:text-current! [&amp;_a]:border-current [&amp;_strong]:text-current! text-yellow-800 dark:text-yellow-300" data-component-part="callout-content"><span data-as="p">The deduplicated per-step values are accurate for input and cache tokens. Per-step <code>output_tokens</code> is a placeholder, so <a href="#read-output-tokens-from-the-result-message" class="link">read output tokens from the result message</a>.</span></div></div>
<span data-as="p">The following example accumulates input tokens across all steps, counting each unique main-loop message ID only once and skipping subagent messages, and reads the output total from the result message, which covers the main loop:</span>
<div class="code-block mt-5 mb-8 not-prose rounded-2xl relative group min-w-0 print:print-color-exact text-gray-950 dark:text-gray-50 codeblock-light border border-gray-950/10 dark:border-white/10 <h3 class="flex whitespace-pre-wrap group font-semibold" id="break-down-usage-per-model" aria-labelledby="_R_2h9deehkd74t9_"><a href="#break-down-usage-per-model" class="anchor-link" aria-label="Navigate to header: Break down usage per model"></a><span id="_R_2h9deehkd74t9_" class="cursor-pointer">Break down usage per model</span></h3>
<span data-as="p">The result message includes <a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/typescript#modelusage" class="link"><code>modelUsage</code></a>, a map of model name to per-model token counts and cost. This is useful when you run multiple models (for example, Haiku for subagents and Opus for the main agent) and want to see where tokens are going.</span>
<span data-as="p">Each entry’s <code>costBasis</code> says which price table priced that model’s latest request: <code>list</code> for list price, <code>managed</code> for a <a href="/docs/en/settings-reference#modelpricing" class="link"><code>modelPricing</code></a> table, or <code>unknown</code> when neither matched the model ID. The field requires Claude Code v2.1.246 or later.</span>
<span data-as="p">The following example runs a query and prints the cost and token breakdown for each model used:</span>
<div class="code-block mt-5 mb-8 not-prose rounded-2xl relative group min-w-0 print:print-color-exact text-gray-950 dark:text-gray-50 codeblock-light border border-gray-950/10 dark:border-white/10 <h2 class="flex whitespace-pre-wrap group font-semibold" id="accumulate-costs-across-multiple-calls" aria-labelledby="_R_2r9deehkd74t9_"><a href="#accumulate-costs-across-multiple-calls" class="anchor-link" aria-label="Navigate to header: Accumulate costs across multiple calls"></a><span id="_R_2r9deehkd74t9_" class="cursor-pointer">Accumulate costs across multiple calls</span></h2>
<span data-as="p">Each <code>query()</code> call returns <code>total_cost_usd</code> on its results. How you combine the values depends on whether the calls share a session:</span>
<ul>
<li><strong>Independent calls, with no <code>resume</code> or <code>continue</code> option</strong>: each result covers only its own call, so add the totals yourself, as the examples below do.</li>
<li><strong>Calls that resume the same session</strong>: Claude Code saves the session’s totals to its <a href="/docs/en/sessions#where-transcripts-are-stored" class="link">transcript</a> when the process exits normally and restores them when a later call resumes or forks the session. Each result already includes the session’s earlier spend. Read the latest result for the session total; summing results double-counts the restored spend. Before v2.1.277, a session that you resumed through the SDK or <code>claude -p</code> started its totals at zero, so each call’s results covered only that call.</li>
</ul>
<span data-as="p">Either way, the combined figure is still a <a href="#estimates-not-billing" class="link">client-side estimate</a>.</span>
<span data-as="p">In streaming input mode, read each call’s total as described in <a href="#track-costs-in-streaming-input-mode" class="link">Track costs in streaming input mode</a>. For a call that ended in a crash, see <a href="#recover-totals-after-a-session-crash" class="link">Recover totals after a session crash</a>.</span>
<span data-as="p">The following examples run two <code>query()</code> calls sequentially, add each call’s <code>total_cost_usd</code> to a running total, and print both the per-call and combined cost:</span>
<div data-orientation="horizontal" data-activation-direction="none" class="code-group p-0.5 mt-5 mb-8 flex flex-col not-prose relative rounded-2xl border border-gray-950/10 dark:border-white/10 <h2 class="flex whitespace-pre-wrap group font-semibold" id="handle-errors-caching-and-output-token-counts" aria-labelledby="_R_399deehkd74t9_"><a href="#handle-errors-caching-and-output-token-counts" class="anchor-link" aria-label="Navigate to header: Handle errors, caching, and output token counts"></a><span id="_R_399deehkd74t9_" class="cursor-pointer">Handle errors, caching, and output token counts</span></h2>
<span data-as="p">For accurate cost tracking, account for the placeholder output count on assistant messages, the tokens a failed conversation consumed, and cache token pricing.</span>
<h3 class="flex whitespace-pre-wrap group font-semibold" id="read-output-tokens-from-the-result-message" aria-labelledby="_R_3d9deehkd74t9_"><a href="#read-output-tokens-from-the-result-message" class="anchor-link" aria-label="Navigate to header: Read output tokens from the result message"></a><span id="_R_3d9deehkd74t9_" class="cursor-pointer">Read output tokens from the result message</span></h3>
<span data-as="p">Claude Code builds each assistant message from the usage the API reported when the response began, so the message’s <code>output_tokens</code> is only the count the API had reported at <code>message_start</code>, before the response was generated. One API response can produce several assistant messages, and every one of them carries that same placeholder.</span>
<span data-as="p">The API reports the real output count at the end of the response, and Claude Code adds it to the result message. Read output tokens from the result’s <code>usage</code>, or from <code>modelUsage</code> for a per-model breakdown.</span>
<h3 class="flex whitespace-pre-wrap group font-semibold" id="track-costs-on-failed-conversations" aria-labelledby="_R_3j9deehkd74t9_"><a href="#track-costs-on-failed-conversations" class="anchor-link" aria-label="Navigate to header: Track costs on failed conversations"></a><span id="_R_3j9deehkd74t9_" class="cursor-pointer">Track costs on failed conversations</span></h3>
<span data-as="p">Both success and error result messages include <code>usage</code> and <code>total_cost_usd</code>; in Python both fields are typed as optional, so check that they aren’t <code>None</code> before you read them.</span>
<span data-as="p">If a conversation fails midway, you still consumed tokens up to the point of failure. Read cost data from every result message, whether its <code>subtype</code> is <code>success</code> or one of the error subtypes. On some error results, <code>usage</code> reports less than the call spent:</span>
<ul>
<li><strong><code>error_during_execution</code> after a <a href="#recover-totals-after-a-session-crash" class="link">session crash</a></strong>: every cost field may be zeroed.</li>
<li><strong><code>error_max_budget_usd</code></strong>: <code>usage</code> leaves out the response that crossed the budget, while <code>total_cost_usd</code> and <code>modelUsage</code> include it.</li>
</ul>
<span data-as="p">Where you have the choice, account from <code>total_cost_usd</code> or <code>modelUsage</code> rather than <code>usage</code>.</span>
<h3 class="flex whitespace-pre-wrap group font-semibold" id="recover-totals-after-a-session-crash" aria-labelledby="_R_3t9deehkd74t9_"><a href="#recover-totals-after-a-session-crash" class="anchor-link" aria-label="Navigate to header: Recover totals after a session crash"></a><span id="_R_3t9deehkd74t9_" class="cursor-pointer">Recover totals after a session crash</span></h3>
<span data-as="p">When the Claude Code process crashes, it emits a final <code>error_during_execution</code> result and exits, in single-shot and streaming input mode alike. That result may carry zeroed <code>usage</code>, <code>total_cost_usd</code>, and <code>modelUsage</code>, so recover the call’s totals from what arrived before it. Step 1 recovers the full totals whenever an earlier result exists; the fallback in step 2 recovers only the main loop’s input and cache tokens.</span>
<ol>
<li>Use the result of the turn before the crash. In streaming input mode, it holds the running total described in <a href="#track-costs-in-streaming-input-mode" class="link">Track costs in streaming input mode</a>. Go to step 2 instead when that result can’t help you:<!-- -->
<ul>
<li>The call was single-shot, so no earlier result exists.</li>
<li>The crash happened on the first turn.</li>
<li>The turn before the crash was the <code>/clear</code> itself, so its result covers only the reset.</li>
</ul>
</li>
<li>Sum the <code>usage</code> on the assistant messages instead, counting each API response once, as the <a href="#track-per-step-usage" class="link">Track per-step usage</a> example does. In single-shot mode, sum all of them; in streaming input mode, sum the ones that arrived after the last result. This gives you the main loop’s input and cache tokens. Subagent usage isn’t recoverable this way, and neither are output tokens or USD cost, because <a href="#read-output-tokens-from-the-result-message" class="link">per-step <code>output_tokens</code> is a placeholder</a>.</li>
</ol>
<h3 class="flex whitespace-pre-wrap group font-semibold" id="track-cache-tokens" aria-labelledby="_R_439deehkd74t9_"><a href="#track-cache-tokens" class="anchor-link" aria-label="Navigate to header: Track cache tokens"></a><span id="_R_439deehkd74t9_" class="cursor-pointer">Track cache tokens</span></h3>
<span data-as="p">The Agent SDK automatically uses <a href="https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/build-with-claude/prompt-caching" class="link" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer">prompt caching</a> to reduce costs on repeated content. You do not need to configure caching yourself. The usage object includes two additional fields for cache tracking:</span>
<ul>
<li><code>cache_creation_input_tokens</code>: tokens used to create new cache entries (charged at a higher rate than standard input tokens).</li>
<li><code>cache_read_input_tokens</code>: tokens read from existing cache entries (charged at a reduced rate).</li>
</ul>
<span data-as="p">Track these separately from <code>input_tokens</code> to understand caching savings. In TypeScript, these fields are typed on the <a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/typescript#usage" class="link"><code>Usage</code></a> object. In Python, they appear as keys in the <a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/python#resultmessage" class="link"><code>ResultMessage.usage</code></a> dict (for example, <code>message.usage.get(&quot;cache_read_input_tokens&quot;, 0)</code>).</span>
<h3 class="flex whitespace-pre-wrap group font-semibold" id="extend-the-prompt-cache-ttl-to-one-hour" aria-labelledby="_R_4b9deehkd74t9_"><a href="#extend-the-prompt-cache-ttl-to-one-hour" class="anchor-link" aria-label="Navigate to header: Extend the prompt cache TTL to one hour"></a><span id="_R_4b9deehkd74t9_" class="cursor-pointer">Extend the prompt cache TTL to one hour</span></h3>
<span data-as="p">Your own turns fall in the <a href="/docs/en/prompt-caching#which-ttl-each-request-gets" class="link">main conversation TTL bucket</a>, together with the helpers Claude Code runs inline with them. The requests Claude Code makes outside that conversation, such as <a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/subagents" class="link">subagents</a>, have a <a href="/docs/en/prompt-caching#choose-the-ttl-yourself" class="link">separate TTL control</a>.</span>
<span data-as="p">Cache entries for your own turns use a 5-minute TTL by default when you authenticate with an API key or run on Amazon Bedrock, Google Cloud’s Agent Platform, Microsoft Foundry, or <a href="/docs/en/claude-platform-on-aws" class="link">Claude Platform on AWS</a>. If your workload runs many short sessions against the same system prompt and context with gaps longer than 5 minutes between them, the cache expires between sessions and each new session pays full input price.</span>
<span data-as="p">To request a 1-hour TTL on cache writes, set the <a href="/docs/en/env-vars" class="link"><code>ENABLE_PROMPT_CACHING_1H</code></a> environment variable. You can export it in your shell or container environment, or pass it through <code>options.env</code>.</span>
<span data-as="p">The following example enables 1-hour TTL for an agent running on Amazon Bedrock. Because it sets <code>CLAUDE_CODE_USE_BEDROCK</code>, it requires working AWS credentials for <a href="/docs/en/amazon-bedrock" class="link">Amazon Bedrock</a>; without them the query fails.</span>

<span class="line"><span style="color:#1F2328;--shiki-dark:#9CDCFE">  console</span><span style="color:#1F2328;--shiki-dark:#D4D4D4">.</span><span style="color:#8250DF;--shiki-dark:#DCDCAA">log</span><span style="color:#1F2328;--shiki-dark:#D4D4D4">(</span><span style="color:#1F2328;--shiki-dark:#9CDCFE">message</span><span style="color:#1F2328;--shiki-dark:#D4D4D4">);</span></span>
<span class="line"><span style="color:#1F2328;--shiki-dark:#D4D4D4">}</span></span>
</code></pre></div></div></div></div><div class="code-block-fade-overlay print:hidden" data-fade-overlay="true" aria-hidden="true" style="--fade-color-light:#ffffff;--fade-color-dark:#0B0C0E"></div></div></div></div>
<span data-as="p">Cache writes with a 1-hour TTL are billed at a higher rate than 5-minute writes, so enabling this trades higher write cost for more cache reads. See <a href="https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/build-with-claude/prompt-caching" class="link" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer">prompt caching pricing</a> for details. On a Claude subscription within your plan’s included usage, you get the 1-hour TTL on your own turns, and on some of the helper requests Claude Code makes beside them, without setting this variable, and Claude Code drops those turns to the 5-minute TTL once you’re drawing on <a href="https://support.claude.com/en/articles/12429409-extra-usage-for-paid-claude-plans" class="link" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer">usage credits</a>.</span>
<span data-as="p"><code>ENABLE_PROMPT_CACHING_1H</code> asks for the 1-hour TTL on every request in both buckets. To choose a TTL for each bucket separately, use these controls instead. Each takes <code>5m</code> or <code>1h</code> and takes precedence over <code>ENABLE_PROMPT_CACHING_1H</code>:</span>
<ul>
<li>Main conversation: the <code>CLAUDE_CODE_PROMPT_CACHE_TTL</code> <a href="/docs/en/env-vars" class="link">environment variable</a>, or the <a href="/docs/en/settings-reference#promptcachettl" class="link"><code>promptCacheTtl</code></a> setting</li>
<li>Everything else: the <code>CLAUDE_CODE_SUBAGENT_PROMPT_CACHE_TTL</code> environment variable, or the <a href="/docs/en/settings-reference#subagentpromptcachettl" class="link"><code>subagentPromptCacheTtl</code></a> setting</li>
</ul>
<span data-as="p">Setting <code>promptCacheTtl</code> to <code>1h</code> keeps the 1-hour cache on the main conversation while you’re drawing on usage credits. For the full precedence order, see <a href="/docs/en/prompt-caching#choose-the-ttl-yourself" class="link">choose the TTL yourself</a>.</span>

0.4)}\u0026:active{background-color:hsl(var(--bg-400))}}\u0026.underline{opacity:0.8;text-decoration-line:none;text-underline-offset:3px;transition:all In Python, the `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`AssistantMessage`}),` dataclass exposes the same data directly via `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`message.usage`}),` and `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`message.message_id`}),`. When Claude uses multiple tools in one turn, all messages in that turn share the same ID, so deduplicate by ID to avoid double-counting.`]})}),_jsxs(Step,{title:`The result message provides the cumulative estimate`,children:[_jsxs(_components.p,{children:[`When the `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`query()`}),` call completes, the SDK emits a result message with `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`total_cost_usd`}),` and cumulative `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`usage`}),`, typed as `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`/en/agent-sdk/typescript#sdkresultmessage`,children:_jsx(_components.code,{children:`SDKResultMessage`})}),` in TypeScript and `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`/en/agent-sdk/python#resultmessage`,children:_jsx(_components.code,{children:`ResultMessage`})}),` in Python. If you only need the estimated total, you can ignore the per-step usage and read this single value.`]}),_jsxs(_components.p,{children:[`If you make multiple independent `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`query()`}),` calls, each result reflects only the cost of that individual call. A call that resumes a session also counts the session’s earlier spend.`]}),_jsxs(_components.p,{children:[`In streaming input mode, each turn emits its own result message. See `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`#track-costs-in-streaming-input-mode`,children:`Track costs in streaming input mode`}),` for how to read call totals in that mode.`]})]})]}),`\n`,_jsx(Heading,{level:`2`,id:`track-costs-in-streaming-input-mode`,children:`Track costs in streaming input mode`}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.p,{children:[`In `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`/en/agent-sdk/streaming-vs-single-mode`,children:`streaming input mode`}),`, one `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`query()`}),` call carries multiple user turns and each turn emits its own result message. The result fields differ in scope:`]}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.ul,{children:[`\n`,_jsxs(_components.li,{children:[_jsx(_components.strong,{children:_jsx(_components.code,{children:`usage`})}),`: covers only that turn, and within it only the main agent loop, not any subagents it ran.`]}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.li,{children:[_jsxs(_components.strong,{children:[_jsx(_components.code,{children:`total_cost_usd`}),` and `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`modelUsage`}),`, or `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`model_usage`}),` in Python`]}),`: carry the running total for the whole call so far, plus any spend restored when the call resumed a session.`]}),`\n`]}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.p,{children:[`In a call where your app never sends `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`/clear`}),`, `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`/reset`}),`, or `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`/new`}),`, read the latest result for call totals rather than summing across results.`]}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.p,{children:[`The running totals start over each time your app sends one of those three commands, and inside a `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`query()`}),` call nothing else resets them. Three results matter for your accounting:`]}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.ul,{children:[`\n`,_jsxs(_components.li,{children:[_jsxs(_components.strong,{children:[`The `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`/clear`}),` turn’s own result`]}),`: covers only what has run since the reset, and carries a new `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`session_id`}),`.`]}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.li,{children:[_jsx(_components.strong,{children:`Every later result`}),`: keeps counting from that reset.`]}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.li,{children:[_jsxs(_components.strong,{children:[`The last result before each `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`/clear`})]}),`: holds the total for the turns since the previous reset.`]}),`\n`]}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.p,{children:[`To total the whole call, add the last result from before each `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`/clear`}),` to the call’s final result. Every other result, including the `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`/clear`}),` turn’s own, is superseded by a later one.`]}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.p,{children:[`In TypeScript, the SDK also emits an `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`/en/agent-sdk/typescript#sdkconversationresetmessage`,children:_jsx(_components.code,{children:`SDKConversationResetMessage`})}),` at each reset, so you can detect resets from the stream. In Python, the SDK likewise emits a `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`ConversationResetMessage`}),`. Before Python SDK v0.2.137, the Python iterator dropped that message, so on those versions count the resets yourself from the `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`/clear`}),` turns your app sends.`]}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.p,{children:[_jsx(_components.code,{children:`maxBudgetUsd`}),` (TypeScript) or `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`max_budget_usd`}),` (Python) counts only the call’s own spend: totals restored from a resumed session don’t count against it, and a `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`/clear`}),` starts the budget over.`]}),`\n`,_jsx(Heading,{level:`2`,id:`get-the-total-cost-of-a-query`,children:`Get the total cost of a query`}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.p,{children:[`The result message, typed as `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`/en/agent-sdk/typescript#sdkresultmessage`,children:_jsx(_components.code,{children:`SDKResultMessage`})}),` in TypeScript and `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`/en/agent-sdk/python#resultmessage`,children:_jsx(_components.code,{children:`ResultMessage`})}),` in Python, marks the end of the agent loop for a `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`query()`}),` call. It includes `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`total_cost_usd`}),`, the cumulative estimated cost across all steps in that call. A call that resumes a session also counts the session’s earlier spend. Two caveats apply when you read the value:`]}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.ul,{children:[`\n`,_jsxs(_components.li,{children:[`In Python the field is typed as optional, so check that it isn’t `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`None`}),` before you read it.`]}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.li,{children:[`Success and error results both carry it, though the final result of a `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`#recover-totals-after-a-session-crash`,children:`session crash`}),` may carry it zeroed.`]}),`\n`]}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.p,{children:[`In streaming input mode, read call totals as described in `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`#track-costs-in-streaming-input-mode`,children:`Track costs in streaming input mode`}),`.`]}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.p,{children:[`The three result-level fields differ in what they count when the agent spawns `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`/en/agent-sdk/subagents`,children:`subagents`}),`. message`}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.p,{children:[`Claude Code builds each assistant message from the usage the API reported when the response began, so the message’s `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`output_tokens`}),` is only the count the API had reported at `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`message_start`}),`, before the response was generated. One API response can produce several assistant messages, and every one of them carries that same placeholder.`]}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.p,{children:[`The API reports the real output count at the end of the response, and Claude Code adds it to the result message. Read output tokens from the result’s `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`usage`}),`, or from `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`modelUsage`}),` for a per-model breakdown.`]}),`\n`,_jsx(Heading,{level:`3`,id:`track-costs-on-failed-conversations`,children:`Track costs on failed conversations`}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.p,{children:[`Both success and error result messages include `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`usage`}),` and `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`total_cost_usd`}),`; in Python both fields are typed as optional, so check that they aren’t `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`None`}),` before you read them.`]}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.p,{children:[`If a conversation fails midway, you still consumed tokens up to the point of failure. Read cost data from every result message, whether its `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`subtype`}),` is `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`success`}),` or one of the error subtypes. On some error results, `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`usage`}),` reports less than the call spent:`]}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.ul,{children:[`\n`,_jsxs(_components.li,{children:[_jsxs(_components.strong,{children:[_jsx(_components.code,{children:`error_during_execution`}),` after a `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`#recover-totals-after-a-session-crash`,children:`session crash`})]}),`: every cost field may be zeroed.`]}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.li,{children:[_jsx(_components.strong,{children:_jsx(_components.code,{children:`error_max_budget_usd`})}),`: `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`usage`}),` leaves out the response that crossed the budget, while `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`total_cost_usd`}),` and `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`modelUsage`}),` include it.`]}),`\n`]}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.p,{children:[`Where you have the choice, account from `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`total_cost_usd`}),` or `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`modelUsage`}),` rather than `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`usage`}),`.`]}),`\n`,_jsx(Heading,{level:`3`,id:`recover-totals-after-a-session-crash`,children:`Recover totals after a session crash`}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.p,{children:[`When the Claude Code process crashes, it emits a final `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`error_during_execution`}),` result and exits, in single-shot and streaming input mode alike. That result may carry zeroed `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`usage`}),`, `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`total_cost_usd`}),`, and `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`modelUsage`}),`, so recover the call’s totals from what arrived before it. Step 1 recovers the full totals whenever an earlier result exists; the fallback in step 2 recovers only the main loop’s input and cache tokens.`]}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.ol,{children:[`\n`,_jsxs(_components.li,{children:[`Use the result of the turn before the crash. In streaming input mode, it holds the running total described in `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`#track-costs-in-streaming-input-mode`,children:`Track costs in streaming input mode`}),`. Go to step 2 instead when that result can’t help you:`,`\n`,_jsxs(_components.ul,{children:[`\n`,_jsx(_components.li,{children:`The call was single-shot, so no earlier result exists.`}),`\n`,_jsx(_components.li,{children:`The crash happened on the first turn.`}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.li,{children:[`The turn before the crash was the `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`/clear`}),` itself, so its result covers only the reset.`]}),`\n`]}),`\n`]}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.li,{children:[`Sum the `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`usage`}),` on the assistant messages instead, counting each API response once, as the `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`#track-per-step-usage`,children:`Track per-step usage`}),` example does. In single-shot mode, sum all of them; in streaming input mode, sum the ones that arrived after the last result. This gives you the main loop’s input and cache tokens. Subagent usage isn’t recoverable this way, and neither are output tokens or USD cost, because `,_jsxs(_components.a,{href:`#read-output-tokens-from-the-result-message`,children:[`per-step `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`output_tokens`}),` is a placeholder`]}),`.`]}),`\n`]}),`\n`,_jsx(Heading,{level:`3`,id:`track-cache-tokens`,children:`Track cache tokens`}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.p,{children:[`The Agent SDK automatically uses `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/build-with-claude/prompt-caching`,children:`prompt caching`}),` to reduce costs on repeated content. You do not need to configure caching yourself. The usage object includes two additional fields for cache tracking:`]}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.ul,{children:[`\n`,_jsxs(_components.li,{children:[_jsx(_components.code,{children:`cache_creation_input_tokens`}),`: tokens used to create new cache entries (charged at a higher rate than standard input tokens).`]}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.li,{children:[_jsx(_components.code,{children:`cache_read_input_tokens`}),`: tokens read from existing cache entries (charged at a reduced rate).`]}),`\n`]}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.p,{children:[`Track these separately from `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`input_tokens`}),` to understand caching savings. In TypeScript, these fields are typed on the `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`/en/agent-sdk/typescript#usage`,children:_jsx(_components.code,{children:`Usage`})}),` object. In Python, they appear as keys in the `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`/en/agent-sdk/python#resultmessage`,children:_jsx(_components.code,{children:`ResultMessage.usage`})}),` dict (for example, `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`message.usage.get(\"cache_read_input_tokens\", 0)`}),`).`]}),`\n`,_jsx(Heading,{level:`3`,id:`extend-the-prompt-cache-ttl-to-one-hour`,children:`Extend the prompt cache TTL to one hour`}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.p,{children:[`Your own turns fall in the `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`/en/prompt-caching#which-ttl-each-request-gets`,children:`main conversation TTL bucket`}),`, together with the helpers Claude Code runs inline with them. The requests Claude Code makes outside that conversation, such as `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`/en/agent-sdk/subagents`,children:`subagents`}),`, have a `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`/en/prompt-caching#choose-the-ttl-yourself`,children:`separate TTL control`}),`.`]}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.p,{children:[`Cache entries for your own turns use a 5-minute TTL by default when you authenticate with an API key or run on Amazon Bedrock, Google Cloud’s Agent Platform, Microsoft Foundry, or `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`/en/claude-platform-on-aws`,children:`Claude Platform on AWS`}),`. If your workload runs many short sessions against the same system prompt and context with gaps longer than 5 minutes between them, the cache expires between sessions and each new session pays full input price.`]}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.p,{children:[`To request a 1-hour TTL on cache writes, set the `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`/en/env-vars`,children:_jsx(_components.code,{children:`ENABLE_PROMPT_CACHING_1H`})}),` environment variable. You can export it in your shell or container environment, or pass it through `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`options.env`}),`.`]}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.p,{children:[`The following example enables 1-hour TTL for an agent running on Amazon Bedrock. Because it sets `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`CLAUDE_CODE_USE_BEDROCK`}),`, it requires working AWS credentials for `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`/en/amazon-bedrock`,children:`Amazon Bedrock`}),`;
